Joint assessment of the third audit round — v2: remaining points, root-cause resolution of D1–D5, and the final v14 plan
MD
Joint assessment of the third audit round — v2: remaining points, root-cause resolution of D1–D5, and the final v14 plan
Supersedes versions/audit3_joint_assessment_v1.md for Secs. 2 (D1) and 5; everything else in v1 stands. Nothing in the manuscript, report, supplement or changelog has been edited. All numbers below are reproducible from the scripts and JSON files listed in Sec. 6.

0. What changed since v1 (read this first)
My v1 recommendation for D1 (ν = 1.27(9), "4/3 compatible") is withdrawn. It rested on fixed-set sextic fits that reach ν = 1.30–1.35. Root-cause analysis shows those values are artefacts of two things in our own analysis code, not of the data: (a) an error floor of 10⁻⁴ that let a single point with I₃ ≡ 0 in all 800 trajectories carry 98 % of the fit weight, and (b) a sextic polynomial forced through the saturated area-law tail (points with mean I₃ > −0.01 bits and standard errors of 0.001–0.003), which a polynomial cannot follow without tilting the core of the collapse. With either defect removed the envelope is ν = 1.21–1.29; with a flexible spline scaling function the tail can stay in and the answer is the same. The v13 headline ν = 1.24(7) stands. What does not stand is the v13 justification text (the frozen-ν χ² list at l. 1123, whose point set changes with ν, and "4/3 disfavoured at 1.3σ").
The Jensen misattribution (E1) is resolved with an exact mechanism (tilt lowers the measurement density: p_eff = 0.105 at p = 0.16, L = 8; 0.092 in the thermodynamic limit), verified against an exact transfer-matrix computation and 40 000 Born trajectories.
N3 (Level-B certificate for λ₁) is done, in exact rational arithmetic, at L = 16, 20 (d = 2) and L = 16 (d = 3): enclosures of relative width 2–7 × 10⁻¹⁵.
New exact result, found while certifying λ₁: the complete nonzero spectrum of the compressed n = 2 period map has a closed (free-fermion) form — Sec. 2.6. It reproduces Table benchmark to its last digit, gives the finite-size amplitudes at p_c⁽²⁾ in closed form, and turns the annealed benchmark from "agrees with Houtappel's bulk free energy" into "agrees with an exactly known finite-L spectrum". It is numerically verified (all eigenvalues, L ≤ 18; λ₁ to 3 × 10⁻¹⁴ at L ≤ 32; inside the exact enclosures), not yet proved; a decision on how to present it is needed (D6).

2. Directive 2 — root-cause resolutions
2.1 D1 (ν headline): root cause found in our own weighting and model choice, not in the data
Where the v13 number came from. v13's ν = 1.24(7) is the envelope of windowed quartic collapses (|x| ≤ 0.6, 1, 1.5), L_min = 48/64/96, t = 2L/4L (final2_summary.json: 1.19–1.27, with two small-n t = 4L windows at 1.10–1.12 ± 0.07). None of those windows contains a saturated tail point (checked: no point with s.e. < 0.002 enters any window), so they were never affected by the defects below — by luck, not by design.

Where the 1.30–1.35 values of v1 came from. The Request-15 fixed-set fits (P1: p ∈ [0.145, 0.175]; P2: p ∈ [0.14, 0.18]; sextic F; ± A L^(−ω)) include the area-law tail. Two defects:

(i) Error floor. mipt_fss2/nuprofile floor the standard error at 10⁻⁴. The point (L = 384, p = 0.18, t = 2L) has I₃ = 0 in all 800 trajectories (s.e. = 0 → 10⁻⁴ → weight 10⁸), i.e. 98 % of the total weight of any P2 fit at t = 2L. Fix: floor s.e. ≥ 1/N_traj (audit3_P2_refit.json). After the fix the weight shares are L = 256: 35–38 %, L = 384: 33–35 % (P2) and L = 384: 57–64 % (P1, t = 4L); a systematic floor of 0.003–0.01 bits in quadrature equalises them and leaves ν unchanged to ±0.005. The same 10⁻⁴/10⁻⁶ floors sit in local_crossing, slope_at and collapse_cost (v13 pipeline); they did no harm there, but must be replaced by 1/N in the released analysis code (report v3, E8).
(ii) Saturated tail vs polynomial F. Even with the correct floor, the tail points — t = 2L: (192, 0.18) −0.0065(27), (256, 0.175) −0.0075(30), (256, 0.18) −0.0020(12), (384, 0.175) −0.0075(35), (384, 0.18) 0.0000, (512, 0.17) 0.0000; t = 4L: (256, 0.18), (384, 0.17), (384, 0.175), (384, 0.18) — have 0.3–2 % of trajectories with non-zero I₃ and standard errors of 0.001–0.004 bits. A sextic cannot be flat there and steep at the crossing; it compensates by tilting the core: the χ²/point of the core (|x| ≤ 1) drops from 1.18 to 0.92 (t = 2L) and from 1.48 to 1.11 (t = 4L) when the tail is removed, while the tail points themselves fit fine (χ²/pt 0.8–0.9). The distortion is what pushes ν up:
L ≥ 64, floor 1/N	sextic, tail in	sextic, tail out	spline F (K = 6), tail in	spline F, tail out
P1 t = 2L, no corr / corr	1.254 / 1.298	1.234 / 1.274	1.233 / 1.274	1.233 / 1.265
P1 t = 4L	1.231 / 1.297	1.220 / 1.243	1.211 / 1.226	1.211 / 1.227
P2 t = 2L	1.286 / 1.320	1.258 / 1.275	1.252 / 1.269	1.253 / 1.269
P2 t = 4L	1.278 / 1.337	1.235 / 1.265	1.225 / 1.253	1.223 / 1.248
(audit3_saturated.json, audit3_splineF.json; "tail out" = drop points with mean I₃ > −0.01 bits; thresholds 0.01–0.08 on the non-zero fraction give the same result, logs/mipt_audit3_saturated.log.) The three tail-safe columns agree with each other to ≤ 0.01 and with the v13 windowed collapses; only "sextic, tail in" is an outlier. Conclusion: the 1.30–1.35 values were a model artefact.

Bootstrap (over trajectories) of the tail-safe fits, L ≥ 64 (audit3_tailboot.json, 200 resamples; audit3_splineF_boot.json, 100):
sextic/tail-out: P1 2L 0.1594(1)/1.234(21), corr 0.1598(2)/1.274(31); P1 4L 0.1595(1)/1.220(24), corr 0.1597(2)/1.243(33); P2 2L 0.1595(1)/1.258(15), corr 0.1597(2)/1.275(20); P2 4L 0.1594(1)/1.235(17), corr 0.1598(2)/1.265(21).
spline/tail-in: 1.233(19)/1.274(29); 1.211(23)/1.226(40); 1.252(16)/1.269(22); 1.225(15)/1.253(23); p_c 0.1594–0.1598.

Correction to scaling. With the tail handled, A L^(−ω) is preferred only when L = 48 is retained (ΔAIC −7 to −14), is neutral at L ≥ 64 (ΔAIC −6.6 to +0.1) and disfavoured at L ≥ 96 (+3.6 to +3.9); ω is undetermined (0.3–4, bootstrap s.e. ≈ 1); it raises ν by 0.02–0.04. The v13 sentence "ω ≈ 1.7" for the crossing ladder is the ladder's own exponent (1.6(4)–2.5(8) by L_min) and stays as ω_eff.

Like-for-like frozen-ν profiles (fixed point sets, so Δχ² is meaningful; audit3_profiles_floorN.json, audit3_splineF_boot.json), L ≥ 64, spline F with tail in, over P1/P2 × t = 2L/4L × ± correction: Δχ²(ν = 1) = 52–334; Δχ²(1.20) = 0.2–11; Δχ²(1.24) = 0.1–2.6; Δχ²(1.28) = 0–9.9; Δχ²(4/3) = 3.0–36.5; Δχ²(1.40) = 12.5–89. Sextic with tail out: Δχ²(ν = 1) ≥ 72, Δχ²(4/3) = 2.8–31.5.

Resolution (recommended):

Headline ν = 1.24(7) unchanged. The interval covers the full tail-safe envelope 1.21–1.29 including the correction-term variants, the bootstrap errors (0.015–0.04), and the v13 windowed values; the two t = 4L small-window values 1.10–1.12(7) are within 2σ. A tighter 1.24(5) is defensible from the central-value envelope alone; I do not recommend it because the ±0.03 model dependence (window / tail / F / correction) is systematic.
Replace the v13 justification: (a) delete the frozen-ν χ²/dof list "5.3, 2.7, 1.6, 2.1, 5.5" (its point set changes with ν, so the numbers are not comparable — the same artefact I flagged for the |x|-cut profiles) and give the fixed-set Δχ² profile above; (b) replace "4/3 disfavoured at 1.3σ" by: ν = 1 is excluded (Δχ² ≥ 52 in every variant); ν = 4/3 is disfavoured (Δχ² ≥ 3 in every variant, ≥ 20 without a correction term) but not excluded by the weakest variant; (c) state the correction-to-scaling result (one sentence); (d) state the weighting rule (s.e. ≥ 1/N; saturated points excluded or a flexible F) — this is the honest disclosure of the defect and its fix; (e) state that all locators share trajectories (R2).
Slope method: keep as consistency check only (1.13–1.36 as p_c varies over 0.1585–0.1605; it is p_c-sensitive, Δν ≈ 0.1 per 0.0008).
2.2 D2 (p_c headline): keep 0.1595(10)
Tail-safe fixed-set fits 0.1594–0.1599; v13 windowed 0.1589–0.1595; crossing plateau 0.1599(3); ladder extrapolations 0.1600(4)–0.1602(6); joint entropy fit 0.1598–0.1601; ΔS crossings 0.159–0.161. Everything lies inside 0.1595(10), as do Sierant 0.15995(10) and Gullans–Huse 0.1593(5). Re-centering to 0.1597(8) would be cosmetic; not recommended.

2.3 E1 / D5 (Jensen → tilt): mechanism verified exactly
For stabilizer states tr ρ_A² = 2^(−S_A), so P̄_{2,A}/Z̄₂ = E_tilt[2^(−S_A)] with the tilt measure ∝ p_R² (record collision weight), and Jensen gives S̃₂(A) = −log E_tilt[2^(−S_A)] ≤ E_tilt[S_A] — for a common measure the annealed quantity is the smaller one. The observed S̃₂ > E_Born S therefore needs a change of measure, and the p_R² weight supplies it: it favours records with fewer measurements. Exact tilted density p_eff(p) = p + p(1−p) ∂_p log λ₁/(2L): 0.1049 (L = 8), 0.1011 (L = 12), 0.0920 (L → ∞) at p = 0.16; 0.1492 at p = p_c⁽²⁾ (closed form of Sec. 2.6 confirms the transfer-matrix values to 5 digits).

Exact/sampled chain at L = 8, t = 4, p = 0.16 (audit3_tiltchain.json; 40 000 Born trajectories, ESS 5935): Z̄₂ exact 1.4796 × 10⁻² vs sampled E 2^(−N_rand) 1.4942 × 10⁻²; S̃₂ = 2.155 bits; E_tilt S = 2.429; E_Born S(p = 0.16) = 2.019(4); E_Born S(p_eff = 0.1049) = 2.339(4). Chain: 2.019 (Born at p) < 2.339 (Born at the tilted density) ≈ 2.429 (tilt) ≥ 2.155 (Jensen) > 2.019. The tilt gain (+0.41 bits) exceeds the Jensen loss (−0.27 bits); the density shift alone accounts for ≈ 80 % of the gain, the remainder is record selection at fixed density. At L = 12 the exact numbers (S̃₂ = 3.128, E_Born S(p) = 2.394(5), E_Born S(p_eff = 0.1011) = 2.941(4)) show the same ordering; the sampled tilt average there (ESS 454) is not reliable and is not used.

Note for the text: the bare p at which p_eff = 0.1595 is 0.2446 (thermodynamic limit) — close to, but not equal to, p_c⁽²⁾ = 0.2338. This is a numerical coincidence and must not be presented as a mapping between the two transitions.

Placement (D5): App. B l. 1127 (replace "(Jensen)" by the mechanism, two sentences) and one clause at l. 541 (main text) — recommended both.

2.4 D3 (new runs)
N1 (more samples at L = 384/512): not merited. The ν uncertainty is systematic (model/tail), not statistical; the bootstrap errors are already 0.015–0.04.
N2 (purification / reference-qubit locator): merited only if the paper wants to satisfy GATE 4 (independent trajectories, different observable). It is not needed for the correctness of v13/v14. Cost: extend mipt_clifford_sim.py to 2L qubits (system + reference in Bell pairs, circuit and measurements on the system only, record S(reference) = S(system) at t = L, 2L, 4L), validate at p = 0 (S = L) and p = 1 (S = 0 after one layer), L = 16–256, 9 values of p in [0.14, 0.18], 2000 samples: ≈ 2–4× the per-sample cost of the existing runs, i.e. several hours in the background across turns; compare with Gullans–Huse 0.1593(5), ν = 1.28(2). Decision requested (Q3).
N3 (Level-B λ₁ certificate): done. Exact-rational Collatz–Wielandt enclosures of λ₁ for the compressed operator (mipt_audit3_cw_exact.py, audit3_cw_exact.json): L = 16, d = 2: p = 2/5 → [0.0022886508026864124561, 0.0022886508026864175903]; p = 4/25 → [0.075189633382987646, 0.075189633382987911]; p = 1169/5000 → [0.0236927451718797476, 0.0236927451718798042]; L = 20, d = 2, p = 2/5 → [0.00050028570068131041, 0.00050028570068131407]; L = 16, d = 3, p = 2/5 → [6.3658872262159916 × 10⁻⁵, 6.3658872262160092 × 10⁻⁵]. Relative widths 2.2–7.3 × 10⁻¹⁵. These are theorems (positive matrix, Collatz–Wielandt, exact arithmetic on the rational matrix of Eq. M1M2). They belong in App. A next to the rank certificates, and make the benchmark sentence "agrees with Houtappel to 3 × 10⁻⁷" a certified statement at these points.
2.5 Summary of what D1–D5 change in the manuscript
Nothing exact changes. Two numbers are unchanged (p_c, ν, α all stand). Text changes: l. 1123 (justification of ν, weighting disclosure, correction to scaling, 4/3 statement, shared-trajectory statement), l. 1127 (tilt mechanism), l. 541 (one clause), l. 1111 (seed sentence; "720 symplectic actions" clause), l. 1121 (ω_eff), l. 1125 (s₁ clause), l. 1129 (Gullans–Huse values), App. A (λ₁ enclosures), Table numerics caption (bootstrap/floor note), abstract/conclusion: none (ν, p_c unchanged).

2.6 New exact result (D6): closed-form spectrum of the compressed period map
Let K_d, K_h, W₀ be as in Eq. Kdh, m = L/2, β_d = (d²−1)/(d²+1). Define for k ∈ [0, 2π)

Q(k) = cosh²2K_d cosh 2K_h + sinh²2K_d sinh 2K_h − sinh 2K_h cos k, R(k) = 2 sinh 2K_d cos(k/2), w_k^± = Q(k) ± √(Q(k)² − R(k)²).

Then (numerically verified statement) every nonzero eigenvalue of C_comp is

λ = (2 W₀²)^m ∏_{k∈K_σ} w_k^{±},

where in the flip-even sector K₊ = {(2j−1)π/m : j = 1…m} (antiperiodic) with an even number of "−" choices, and in the flip-odd sector K₋ = {2πj/m : j = 0…m−1} (periodic) with an even number of "−" choices for p < p_c⁽²⁾ and an odd number for p > p_c⁽²⁾ (the k = 0 mode changes sign at the transition, exactly as in Kaufman's treatment). At k = π, R = 0 so w_π^− = 0: every state occupying the k = π mode lies in the kernel; the sector containing k = π has 2^(m−2) nonzero states and the other 2^(m−1), total 3·2^(m−2) — Theorem rank (iv) is recovered by mode counting.

Consequences, all checked: (a) a − c = 2b ⇔ Q(0) = R(0) (Q(0) − R(0) ∝ (a − c − 2b)²): the k = 0 mode is gapless exactly at p_c⁽²⁾; (b) (1/L) log λ₁ → log W₀ + f_H (Houtappel) — at p = 0.40: 0.683844659 at L ≥ 64 vs 0.6838449 (L = 28) quoted in the text; (c) at p_c⁽²⁾ the scaled gaps have exact limits L log(λ₁/λ_odd)/2π → β_d/4 and L log(λ₁/λ_ε)/2π → 2β_d (symbolically: ac/b² = β_d² at θ_c), ratio 1/8 = x_σ/x_ε for every d; (d) the closed form reproduces the crossings of Table benchmark to ≤ 5 × 10⁻⁶ (the table's rounding) and its ratio row to all four decimals; (e) E^T = E τ with τ the bond-ring shift, so C_comp = W₀^L (E D_h)² τ.

Verification: dense sector spectra vs closed form, all nonzero eigenvalues, m = 4–9 (L = 8–18), d = 2, 3, 5, p ∈ {0.01, 0.05, 0.16, 0.22, p_c⁽²⁾ ± 2 × 10⁻⁴, 0.25, 0.30, 0.45, 0.5, 0.6, 0.75, 0.8}: |Δ log λ| ≤ 10⁻¹⁰ (limited by the dense eigensolver on eigenvalues 10⁻¹² λ₁); at p = 0.85–0.99 the top eigenvalues (down to 10⁻⁹ λ₁) agree to 10⁻⁷–10⁻⁹ while the deep tail is below double precision; λ₁ at L = 28, 32 (p = 0.16, p_c⁽²⁾, 0.30) agrees with sparse power iteration to 3 × 10⁻¹⁴; the closed form lies inside every exact-rational enclosure of N3. The manuscript already states the mechanism (l. 538: triangular-lattice Ising, free-fermion/Pfaffian for every sign pattern; Kaufman 1949, Newell 1950, Stephenson); the specific product formula for this transfer direction (the cos(k/2) of the half-shift E^T = Eτ) is what is new here. It is a conjecture with an overwhelming numerical basis and a standard proof route (Kaufman's spinor analysis of E D_h with the parity-dependent boundary condition, which the OBC test confirms: E and D_h are exactly Gaussian in the Jordan–Wigner representation with open ends, residual 10⁻¹⁶, and fail only through the PBC boundary term).

Options (Q2): (a) add as a Remark after Prop. houtappel, explicitly labelled "closed form, verified numerically as stated, not proved here", with the exact amplitudes and the reproduction of Table benchmark — 10–12 lines; (b) prove it as a Proposition — a 2–3 page spinor derivation, feasible but a separate task; (c) omit and keep it in the report/supplement.

Done in this round (cheap, all on exact objects or existing data): N3 certificates; closed-form spectrum verification; the tail/spline/bootstrap analyses; the L = 8/12 tilt chain (40 000 + 40 000 trajectories, 9 s); bit-exact seed regeneration test. Not run: N1 (not merited), N2 (decision Q3). No background process is running.

mipt_purif_sim.py

mipt_purif_sim.py
PYTHON
"""Purification (mixed-initial-state) locator of the Clifford MIPT -- Gullans-Huse protocol, own stabilizer simulator.

Protocol (Gullans & Huse, PRX 10, 041020 (2020), Sec. VI.2): the L-qubit system starts in the completely mixed
state, realised here as L Bell pairs between system qubit i and a reference qubit L+i (2L qubits in total, pure).
The circuit (identical ensemble to mipt_clifford_sim.py: PBC brickwork, one period = [measurement layer][gates on
odd matching][measurement layer][gates on even matching], every site measured in Z with probability p per layer,
gates = uniform Sp(4,2) elements) and all measurements act on the SYSTEM only.  The reference is never touched, so
S(reference) = S(system mixed state) is the purification entropy; it is L bits at p = 0 for all t, and 0 after the
first layer at p = 1.

Recorded at chosen times t (periods), integer bits:
   col 0  S_ref            (= entropy of the system's mixed state, the Gullans-Huse order parameter)
   col 1  S_A              (A = system sites 0..L/2-1)
   col 2  S_B              (B = system sites L/2..L-1)
   col 3  S_sys            (must equal col 0; consistency check of the tableau)
   col 4  N_rand           (cumulative number of random measurement outcomes)
   col 5  N_meas           (cumulative number of measurements)
Bipartite mutual information of the mixed state: I(A:B) = S_A + S_B - S_sys.

Outcome signs are irrelevant for every quantity above (all are stabilizer ranks), and every random outcome has
probability 1/2, so the sample is Born-distributed for the recorded data.

Files: outdir/purif_L{L}_p{p:.4f}_c{chunk:02d}.npz with data[nsamp, ntimes, 6], rec, L, p, seed0, chunk.
Trajectory k of a chunk is regenerated bit-exactly by run_sample_purif(L, p, rec, SP4, seed0 + k).
"""
import numpy as np, numba as nb, sys, time, os
from mipt_clifford_sim import SP4, _gate, _measure, _entropy, seed_for

@nb.njit(cache=True)
def _init_bell(L, W):
    """2L-qubit tableau (columns = Pauli components x_0..x_{2L-1}, z_0..z_{2L-1}; bit r of a column = generator r)
    stabilising L Bell pairs (i, L+i): generators X_i X_{L+i} (index 2i) and Z_i Z_{L+i} (index 2i+1)."""
    n = 2 * L
    cols = np.zeros((2 * n, W), dtype=np.uint64)
    for i in range(L):
        g = 2 * i
        bit = np.uint64(1) << np.uint64(g & 63)
        cols[i, g >> 6] |= bit
        cols[L + i, g >> 6] |= bit
        g = 2 * i + 1
        bit = np.uint64(1) << np.uint64(g & 63)
        cols[n + i, g >> 6] |= bit
        cols[n + L + i, g >> 6] |= bit
    return cols

@nb.njit(cache=True)
def run_sample_purif(L, p, rec_times, G, seed):
    np.random.seed(seed)
    n = 2 * L
    W = (n + 63) >> 6
    cols = _init_bell(L, W)
    nrec = rec_times.shape[0]
    res = np.zeros((nrec, 6))
    nrand = 0; nmeas = 0; ir = 0
    Tmax = rec_times[nrec - 1]
    half = L // 2
    ref = np.arange(L, n); sysA = np.arange(0, half); sysB = np.arange(half, L); sysall = np.arange(0, L)
    for t in range(1, Tmax + 1):
        for layer in range(2):
            for s in range(L):
                if np.random.random() < p:
                    nmeas += 1
                    nrand += _measure(cols, n, W, s)
            for b in range(half):
                i = (2 * b + layer) % L; j = (2 * b + 1 + layer) % L
                g = np.random.randint(720)
                _gate(cols, n, W, i, j, G[g])
        if ir < nrec and t == rec_times[ir]:
            res[ir, 0] = _entropy(cols, n, W, ref)
            res[ir, 1] = _entropy(cols, n, W, sysA)
            res[ir, 2] = _entropy(cols, n, W, sysB)
            res[ir, 3] = _entropy(cols, n, W, sysall)
            res[ir, 4] = nrand; res[ir, 5] = nmeas
            ir += 1
    return res

def run_chunk(L, p, rec_times, nsamp, seed0, chunk, outdir):
    rec = np.array(rec_times, dtype=np.int64)
    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
    fname = os.path.join(outdir, f"purif_L{L}_p{p:.4f}_c{chunk:02d}.npz")
    data = np.zeros((nsamp, len(rec), 6), dtype=np.int32)
    t0 = time.time()
    for k in range(nsamp):
        data[k] = run_sample_purif(L, p, rec, SP4, seed0 + k)
    np.savez_compressed(fname, data=data, rec=rec, L=L, p=p, seed0=seed0, chunk=chunk)
    return time.time() - t0, fname

def job_list(spec, chunk_size=500):
    """spec: list of (L, nsamp, [p...], [tfactors]). Returns jobs (L, p, rec, nsamp_chunk, seed0, chunk, key)."""
    used = []; jobs = []
    for (L, nsamp, ps, tf) in spec:
        rec = sorted(set(int(round(f * L)) for f in tf))
        for p in ps:
            seed0 = seed_for(L, p, nsamp, used) + 500_000_000  # disjoint from the pure-state seed blocks
            nch = (nsamp + chunk_size - 1) // chunk_size
            for c in range(nch):
                n_c = min(chunk_size, nsamp - c * chunk_size)
                jobs.append((L, p, rec, n_c, seed0 + c * chunk_size, c))
    return jobs

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # usage: python mipt_purif_sim.py outdir wid nworkers [spec name]
    outdir = sys.argv[1]; wid = int(sys.argv[2]); nw = int(sys.argv[3])
    specname = sys.argv[4] if len(sys.argv) > 4 else "main"
    P9 = [0.14, 0.145, 0.15, 0.155, 0.16, 0.165, 0.17, 0.175, 0.18]
    T4 = [0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4]
    if specname == "main":
        spec = [(16, 4000, P9, T4), (32, 4000, P9, T4), (64, 3000, P9, T4), (128, 2000, P9, T4),
                (256, 2000, P9, [0.25, 0.5, 1, 2])]
    elif specname == "valid":
        spec = [(16, 200, [0.0, 1.0], T4), (32, 100, [0.0, 1.0], T4)]
    elif specname == "bench":
        spec = [(64, 20, [0.16], T4), (128, 10, [0.16], T4), (256, 4, [0.16], [0.25, 0.5, 1, 2])]
    else:
        raise SystemExit("unknown spec")
    jobs = job_list(spec)
    mine = [j for (k, j) in enumerate(jobs) if k % nw == wid]
    print(f"worker {wid}/{nw}: {len(mine)} of {len(jobs)} jobs", flush=True)
    for (L, p, rec, n_c, seed0, c) in mine:
        fname = os.path.join(outdir, f"purif_L{L}_p{p:.4f}_c{c:02d}.npz")
        if os.path.exists(fname):
            print(f"skip {fname}", flush=True); continue
        dt, fname = run_chunk(L, p, rec, n_c, seed0, c, outdir)
        print(f"L={L} p={p} chunk={c} n={n_c} rec={rec} seed0={seed0}: {dt:.1f}s ({dt/n_c*1e3:.1f} ms/sample) -> {fname}", flush=True)

mipt_purif_analysis.py
135 lines
open
mipt_purif_analysis.py
PYTHON
"""Analysis of the purification (mixed-initial-state) locator run, mipt_data_purif/ (see mipt_purif_sim.py).
Order parameter: average reference entropy <S_ref>(p, L, tau=t/L) = <S(rho_system)> (Gullans-Huse PRX 10, 041020).
Scaling ansatz (GH Eq. 37, z=1): <S> = F(x, tau), x = (p - p_c) L^{1/nu}.
Outputs mipt_results/purif_summary.json and prints crossings, collapse fits and bootstrap errors.
Standard errors are floored at 1/N_traj (integer-valued observable; a zero sample variance is not a zero error)."""
import numpy as np, glob, json, sys, itertools, collections
from scipy.optimize import minimize, brentq

TAUS = [0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]

def load():
    acc = collections.defaultdict(list)
    for f in sorted(glob.glob('mipt_data_purif/purif_L*_c*.npz')):
        z = np.load(f); L = int(z['L']); p = float(z['p']); rec = z['rec']
        acc[(L, p)].append((rec, z['data']))
    out = {}
    for (L, p), lst in acc.items():
        rec = lst[0][0]; data = np.concatenate([d for (_, d) in lst], axis=0)
        assert all(np.array_equal(r, rec) for (r, _) in lst)
        out[(L, p)] = (rec, data)
    return out

def stats(D):
    """mean, se (floored at 1/N), N of S_ref for each (L, p, tau)."""
    T = {}
    for (L, p), (rec, data) in D.items():
        for it, t in enumerate(rec):
            tau = t / L
            S = data[:, it, 0].astype(float)
            N = len(S); m = S.mean(); se = max(S.std(ddof=1) / np.sqrt(N), 1.0 / N)
            IAB = (data[:, it, 1] + data[:, it, 2] - data[:, it, 3]).astype(float)
            T[(L, p, round(tau, 4))] = dict(mean=m, se=se, N=N, IAB=IAB.mean(), IAB_se=max(IAB.std(ddof=1) / np.sqrt(N), 1.0 / N),
                                            nonzero=float((S > 0).mean()))
    return T

def crossings(T, Ls, ps, tau):
    """Pairwise crossing p*(L1,L2) of <S_ref>(p) using local cubic interpolation of the two curves."""
    out = []
    for L1, L2 in zip(Ls[:-1], Ls[1:]):
        y1 = np.array([T[(L1, p, tau)]['mean'] for p in ps]); y2 = np.array([T[(L2, p, tau)]['mean'] for p in ps])
        c1 = np.polyfit(ps, y1, 3); c2 = np.polyfit(ps, y2, 3)
        f = lambda p: np.polyval(c1, p) - np.polyval(c2, p)
        grid = np.linspace(ps[0], ps[-1], 401); vals = f(grid)
        roots = [brentq(f, grid[i], grid[i + 1]) for i in range(len(grid) - 1) if vals[i] * vals[i + 1] < 0]
        out.append((L1, L2, roots[0] if len(roots) == 1 else (roots if roots else None)))
    return out

def collapse(T, Ls, ps, tau, K=6, x_max=None, nu0=1.25, pc0=0.16, fixed=None):
    """Least-squares collapse S = F(x) with F a polynomial of degree K in x; returns (pc, nu, chi2, npts)."""
    pts = [(L, p, T[(L, p, tau)]['mean'], T[(L, p, tau)]['se']) for L in Ls for p in ps]
    Lv = np.array([q[0] for q in pts], float); pv = np.array([q[1] for q in pts]); yv = np.array([q[2] for q in pts]); ev = np.array([q[3] for q in pts])
    def chi2(par):
        pc, nu = par
        x = (pv - pc) * Lv ** (1.0 / nu)
        sel = np.ones(len(x), bool) if x_max is None else (np.abs(x) <= x_max)
        if sel.sum() < K + 4: return 1e9
        A = np.vander(x[sel], K + 1) / ev[sel, None]
        b = yv[sel] / ev[sel]
        coef, *_ = np.linalg.lstsq(A, b, rcond=None)
        return float(np.sum((A @ coef - b) ** 2))
    if fixed is not None:
        pc, nu = fixed
        return pc, nu, chi2((pc, nu)), int(np.sum(np.abs((pv - pc) * Lv ** (1 / nu)) <= (x_max if x_max else 1e9)))
    best = None
    for nu_s in (1.1, 1.25, 1.4):
        for pc_s in (0.158, 0.160, 0.162):
            r = minimize(chi2, [pc_s, nu_s], method='Nelder-Mead', options=dict(xatol=1e-6, fatol=1e-8, maxiter=4000))
            if best is None or r.fun < best.fun: best = r
    pc, nu = best.x
    npts = int(np.sum(np.abs((pv - pc) * Lv ** (1 / nu)) <= (x_max if x_max else 1e9)))
    return float(pc), float(nu), float(best.fun), npts

def bootstrap_collapse(D, Ls, ps, tau, nboot, K=6, x_max=None, seed=1):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    res = []
    for b in range(nboot):
        Tb = {}
        for L in Ls:
            for p in ps:
                rec, data = D[(L, p)]
                it = [i for i, t in enumerate(rec) if abs(t / L - tau) < 1e-9][0]
                S = data[:, it, 0].astype(float); N = len(S)
                idx = rng.integers(0, N, N); Sb = S[idx]
                Tb[(L, p, tau)] = dict(mean=Sb.mean(), se=max(S.std(ddof=1) / np.sqrt(N), 1.0 / N))
        res.append(collapse(Tb, Ls, ps, tau, K=K, x_max=x_max)[:2])
    res = np.array(res)
    return res.mean(0), res.std(0, ddof=1)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    nboot = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 0
    D = load(); T = stats(D)
    Ls = sorted(set(L for (L, p) in D)); ps = sorted(set(p for (L, p) in D))
    taus = sorted(set(k[2] for k in T))
    summary = dict(Ls=Ls, ps=ps, taus=taus, table={}, crossings={}, collapses={}, frozen={}, boot={})
    print('sizes', Ls, 'p', ps, 'taus', taus)
    for tau in taus:
        print(f'\n=== tau = t/L = {tau} ===')
        print('p      ' + ''.join(f'L={L:<4d}          ' for L in Ls if (Ls[0], ps[0], tau) in T and (L, ps[0], tau) in T))
        for p in ps:
            row = []
            for L in Ls:
                if (L, p, tau) in T:
                    s = T[(L, p, tau)]; row.append(f"{s['mean']:.4f}({s['se']:.4f}) ")
                    summary['table'][f'{L}_{p}_{tau}'] = dict(mean=s['mean'], se=s['se'], N=s['N'], IAB=s['IAB'], IAB_se=s['IAB_se'], nonzero=s['nonzero'])
            print(f'{p:.3f}  ' + ''.join(row))
        Lt = [L for L in Ls if (L, ps[0], tau) in T]
        if len(Lt) < 2: continue
        cr = crossings(T, Lt, ps, tau)
        print('crossings p*(L1,L2):', [(a, b, (round(c, 5) if isinstance(c, float) else c)) for (a, b, c) in cr])
        summary['crossings'][str(tau)] = [(a, b, c) for (a, b, c) in cr]
        for Lmin in (16, 32, 64):
            Lset = [L for L in Lt if L >= Lmin]
            if len(Lset) < 3: continue
            for xm in (None, 2.0):
                pc, nu, chi2, npts = collapse(T, Lset, ps, tau, K=6, x_max=xm)
                dof = npts - 2 - 7
                print(f'  collapse L>={Lmin} |x|<={xm}: p_c={pc:.5f} nu={nu:.4f} chi2/dof={chi2:.1f}/{dof}')
                summary['collapses'][f'{tau}_L{Lmin}_x{xm}'] = dict(pc=pc, nu=nu, chi2=chi2, dof=dof, npts=npts)
                if xm is None:
                    fro = {}
                    for nuf in (1.0, 1.2, 1.24, 1.28, 4 / 3, 1.4):
                        # profile over p_c at frozen nu on the same (full) point set
                        r = minimize(lambda q: collapse(T, Lset, ps, tau, K=6, x_max=None, fixed=(q[0], nuf))[2], [pc], method='Nelder-Mead', options=dict(xatol=1e-6, fatol=1e-8))
                        fro[round(nuf, 4)] = dict(pc=float(r.x[0]), dchi2=float(r.fun - chi2))
                    print('   frozen-nu dchi2 (full set):', {k: round(v['dchi2'], 1) for k, v in fro.items()})
                    summary['frozen'][f'{tau}_L{Lmin}'] = fro
        if nboot and tau in (1.0, 2.0):
            for Lmin in (32, 64):
                Lset = [L for L in Lt if L >= Lmin]
                mean, sd = bootstrap_collapse(D, Lset, ps, tau, nboot, K=6, x_max=None)
                print(f'  bootstrap L>={Lmin} (n={nboot}): p_c={mean[0]:.5f}+-{sd[0]:.5f} nu={mean[1]:.4f}+-{sd[1]:.4f}')
                summary['boot'][f'{tau}_L{Lmin}'] = dict(pc=float(mean[0]), pc_sd=float(sd[0]), nu=float(mean[1]), nu_sd=float(sd[1]), nboot=nboot)
    json.dump(summary, open('mipt_results/purif_summary.json', 'w'), indent=1, default=float)
    print('\nwritten mipt_results/purif_summary.json')

C = E D_h Eᵀ D_h: since E is invertible on the sector except at the kernel, the spectrum of C on the non-kernel part = spec of R_C(k) blocks: 2×2 momentum block with eigenvalues w⁺/w⁻, w⁻/w⁺ → the product formula.

\begin{proposition}[Closed-form spectrum of the compressed two-replica period map] \label{prop:spectrum} Let L=2m, d≥2, 0<p<1, and let C_comp be the compressed operator of Theorem \ref{thm:compression}, with K_d, K_h, W_0 as in Eq.~\eqref{eq:Kdh} and β_d=(d²−1)/(d²+1). Define, for k∈0,2π), Q(k) = cosh²(2K_d) cosh(2K_h) + sinh²(2K_d) sinh(2K_h) − sinh(2K_h) cos k, R(k) = 2 sinh(2K_d) cos(k/2), w_k^± = Q(k) ± √(Q(k)² − R(k)²). Then every nonzero eigenvalue of C_comp is of the form λ = (2W_0²)^m ∏_{k∈K_σ} w_k^{±_k}, where the product runs over the states of the flip sector σ: in the flip-even sector K₊ = {(2j−1)π/m : j=1,…,m} with an even number of "−" choices, and in the flip-odd sector K₋ = {2πj/m : j=0,…,m−1} with an even number of "−" choices for p < p_c^{(2)}(d) and an odd number for p > p_c^{(2)}(d). In particular w_π^− = 0, so the kernel has dimension 2^{m−2} and rank C_comp = 3·2^{m−2}, recovering Theorem rank(iv) by mode counting; at p = p_c^{(2)} the k=0 mode is gapless (w_0^−=0 ⇔ a−c=2b), and the scaled gaps tend to L log(λ₁/λ_odd) → 2πβ_d/4·(2/L?)... [blocked]

For the triangular Ising: correlation length ξ ~ |θ−θ_c|^{-1} (ν=1); the flip-odd gap λ_odd ~ e^{-L/ξ_σ} with ξ_σ = 2π/(β_d)? From the table: ratio row → 0.125 at p_c; gap: L log(λ₁/λ_odd) at p_c: from lib gaps at m=64: I'll compute in the script (A10) to get the actual constants and match to β_d/4... the v2 doc says "L log(λ₁/λ_odd)/2π → β_d/4 and L log(λ₁/λ_ε)/2π → 2β_d". Actually — the Kaufman factorization (from the session memory route): "E = coshK₂·Ỹ + sinhK₂·z_mỸz_1, Ỹ = c e^{K₁x_m}U_{m−1}⋯U_1, U_k = e^{K₂z_kz_{k+1}}c e^{K₁x_k}". Note: U_k = e^{K₂ z_k z_{k+1}} c e^{K₁ x_k}: an e^{K₁x_k} factor (a 1-spin "field" in x_k = 1−2n_k — i.e., a Pauli-x in the occupation basis!) followed by an NN flip coupling. This is the standard "spinor" trick: x_k = 1−2n_k acts as a Jordan-fermion flip, and the e^{K₂ z_kz_{k+1}} terms are the NN Ising couplings. The c = √(2sinh2K₁) factors normalize so that Ỹ becomes the product of 2×2 "spinor" matrices. This is exactly Kaufman's 1949 method (and Newell 1950 for the triangular lattice).

So in the manuscript proof:

Step 1: define the Majorana operators; note E is a sum of two Gaussian terms (the cosh/sinh decomposition) — prove by the spinor-chain identity (induction on k using [z_k, e^{K₂z_kz_{k+1}}] relations and x_k z_k = y_k etc.). The identity E = coshK₂ Ỹ + sinhK₂ z_m Ỹ z_1 is verified to 5e-15.

"""Purification (mixed-initial-state) locator of the Clifford MIPT -- Gullans-Huse protocol, own stabilizer simulator.

Protocol (Gullans & Huse, PRX 10, 041020 (2020), Sec. VI.2): the L-qubit system starts in the completely mixed
state, realised here as L Bell pairs between system qubit i and a reference qubit L+i (2L qubits in total, pure).
The circuit (identical ensemble to mipt_clifford_sim.py: PBC brickwork, one period = [measurement layer][gates on
odd matching][measurement layer][gates on even matching], every site measured in Z with probability p per layer,
gates = uniform Sp(4,2) elements) and all measurements act on the SYSTEM only.  The reference is never touched, so
S(reference) = S(system mixed state) is the purification entropy; it is L bits at p = 0 for all t, and 0 after the
first layer at p = 1.

Recorded at chosen times t (periods), integer bits:
   col 0  S_ref            (= entropy of the system's mixed state, the Gullans-Huse order parameter)
   col 1  S_A              (A = system sites 0..L/2-1)
   col 2  S_B              (B = system sites L/2..L-1)
   col 3  S_sys            (must equal col 0; consistency check of the tableau)
   col 4  N_rand           (cumulative number of random measurement outcomes)
   col 5  N_meas           (cumulative number of measurements)
Bipartite mutual information of the mixed state: I(A:B) = S_A + S_B - S_sys.

Outcome signs are irrelevant for every quantity above (all are stabilizer ranks), and every random outcome has
probability 1/2, so the sample is Born-distributed for the recorded data.

Files: outdir/purif_L{L}_p{p:.4f}_c{chunk:02d}.npz with data[nsamp, ntimes, 6], rec, L, p, seed0, chunk.
Trajectory k of a chunk is regenerated bit-exactly by run_sample_purif(L, p, rec, SP4, seed0 + k).
"""
import numpy as np, numba as nb, sys, time, os
from mipt_clifford_sim import SP4, _gate, _measure, _entropy, seed_for

@nb.njit(cache=True)
def _init_bell(L, W):
    """2L-qubit tableau (columns = Pauli components x_0..x_{2L-1}, z_0..z_{2L-1}; bit r of a column = generator r)
    stabilising L Bell pairs (i, L+i): generators X_i X_{L+i} (index 2i) and Z_i Z_{L+i} (index 2i+1)."""
    n = 2 * L
    cols = np.zeros((2 * n, W), dtype=np.uint64)
    for i in range(L):
        g = 2 * i
        bit = np.uint64(1) << np.uint64(g & 63)
        cols[i, g >> 6] |= bit
        cols[L + i, g >> 6] |= bit
        g = 2 * i + 1
        bit = np.uint64(1) << np.uint64(g & 63)
        cols[n + i, g >> 6] |= bit
        cols[n + L + i, g >> 6] |= bit
    return cols

@nb.njit(cache=True)
def run_sample_purif(L, p, rec_times, G, seed):
    np.random.seed(seed)
    n = 2 * L
    W = (n + 63) >> 6
    cols = _init_bell(L, W)
    nrec = rec_times.shape[0]
    res = np.zeros((nrec, 6))
    nrand = 0; nmeas = 0; ir = 0
    Tmax = rec_times[nrec - 1]
    half = L // 2
    ref = np.arange(L, n); sysA = np.arange(0, half); sysB = np.arange(half, L); sysall = np.arange(0, L)
    for t in range(1, Tmax + 1):
        for layer in range(2):
            for s in range(L):
                if np.random.random() < p:
                    nmeas += 1
                    nrand += _measure(cols, n, W, s)
            for b in range(half):
                i = (2 * b + layer) % L; j = (2 * b + 1 + layer) % L
                g = np.random.randint(720)
                _gate(cols, n, W, i, j, G[g])
        if ir < nrec and t == rec_times[ir]:
            res[ir, 0] = _entropy(cols, n, W, ref)
            res[ir, 1] = _entropy(cols, n, W, sysA)

The proof script (mipt_audit3_gauss_proof.py): design it with the E/D_h definitions:
Basis: index s ∈ {±1}^m as an integer 0..2^m−1, bit i ↔ spin s_{i+1}? I need to be careful about the order. Define: integer n, bits b_0..b_{m-1} (b_0 = bit 0 = least significant). Let spin s_k = +1 if bit_{k-1} = 0, else −1, k=1..m (bit k-1 is the (k−1)-th bit). Build the matrix directly from the formula: (E){s's} = exp[K_d Σ_k s'k (s_k + s{k+1})], s{m+1} = s_1 (PBC). (D_h){ss} = exp[K_h Σ_k s_k s{k+1}]. (C){s's} = W_0^L Σ{t} E_{s't} D_h,t E^T_{tt'} D_h,t' = W_0^L (E D_h Eᵀ D_h)_{s's}. Build E densely: for each s (input row), for each s' (output row): compute the exponent. A vectorized approach: build the 2^m × 2^m matrix with numpy broadcasting over the spin arrays: S[k, s] = spin value. Then E[s', s] = exp(Kd * Σ_k S[k,s']*(S[k,s]+S[(k+1)%m, s])). This is a 3-index O(m·4^m) op — for m=9: 9×512×512 = 2.4M ✓. m up to 9 (512² matrix is fine).

Comparison targets: (A5) the nonzero eigenvalues of C vs the closed form (2W_0²)^m ∏ w_k^{±}: but wait — which overall factor? C = W_0^L E D_h Eᵀ D_h. The lib's λ₁ formula: (2W_0²)^m ∏ w_k^+ — is that for C or for (ED_hEᵀD_h)? I'll verify numerically: compute both and match the factor. The lib's dense() builds C from A1(A2(·)) — A1, A2 from z2_gap_scan.make_ops — probably M1, M2 (with W_0 included). M1M2 = W_0^{2m} E D_h Eᵀ D_h = W_0^L (E D_h Eᵀ D_h) = C ✓. So the lib's formula is for C including the W_0^L factor. In the proof: the nonzero spectrum of C = { (W_0^L)(2W_0²)^m ∏ w_k^{±} } — I'll verify the total prefactor numerically and state it as the product that matches (e.g., λ = (2W_0⁴)^m ∏ w_k^± — check which it is; the v2 doc says λ = (2W₀²)^m ∏ w_k^±, and dense() presumably reproduces C... the v2 doc's "verified statement" says λ = (2 W₀²)^m ∏ w_k^± for C_comp. (A1) The spinor factorization of E: try the parameterizations: K1cand ∈ {Kd, 0.5arctanh(exp(-2Kd)), 0.5arctanh(exp(-2Kh))...}, K2cand ∈ {Kh, Kd, ...}; c = √(2 sinh 2K1); U_k = exp(K2 z_k z_{k+1}) · c · exp(K1 x_k); Ỹ = c exp(K1 x_m) U_{m-1}...U_1 (order: the product U_{m−1}⋯U_1 applied right to left, U_1 first); E? = cosh(K2) Ỹ + sinh(K2) z_m Ỹ z_1. Test the residual for (K1,K2) ∈ {Kd,Kh}×{Kd,Kh} (4 combos) + possibly K1=Kd, K2=Kh. Also test the variant with K1x = arctanh form. Record the best residual. (If none is <1e-13, extend the search: also the variant with e^{K1 x_k} replaced by e^{K1 z_k}?? The route says x_k (1−2n_k) — but note: in the spin basis s_k=±1, the occupation n_k=(1−s_k)/2? Then x_k = 1−2n_k = s_k! Wait: n_k = (1−s_k)/2 → 2n_k = 1−s_k → x_k = 1−2n_k = s_k. Oh — x_k equals the spin s_k itself! Hmm, but the route distinguishes x_k and z_k (the Pauli z). In the spin eigenbasis, z_k = diag(s_k) = x_k = s_k as an operator (both diagonal with eigenvalues ±1). The distinction matters after the Jordan transform (the x in the occupation basis becomes a Majorana flip). For the matrix identity E = coshK₂ Ỹ + sinhK₂ z_m Ỹ z_1, the operators act on the spin Hilbert space C^{2^m}: z_k = diag(s_k), x_k = diag(s_k)?? They're the same matrix! Then Ỹ = c e^{K₁ s_m} U_{m−1}...U_1, U_k = e^{K₂ z_kz_{k+1}} c e^{K₁ s_k} = e^{K₂ s_k s_{k+1}} c e^{K₁ s_k} (all diagonal or NN). Then E? = coshK₂ Ỹ + sinhK₂ s_m Ỹ s_1 — a matrix in the spin basis. (A3) Determinant: for m=4..8, ε=±1: E_ε = P_ε E P_ε (the parity sector ε of ∏ s_k); compute det(E_ε) (sector dim 2^{m−1}); compare det^{1/2^{m−1}} with ρ_ε c^m, ρ_ε² = 1 + ε(−1)^m (sinh2K₂/sinh2K₁)^m — try K1=Kd, K2=Kh. Also the variant with K1/K2 swapped.

(A4) Momentum blocks: C on the sector ε: transform to the basis ψ_q(s) = (1/√m)Σ_s e^{i q s·?}... for a 1D spin chain with PBC, the momentum index k: the eigenvector of the translation T (T s)k = s{k−1}. On the sector ε, T has eigenvalues e^{ik} with k ∈ {2πj/m} (periodic) — but the route says the sector ε=+1 (flip-even) has k ∈ (2j−1)π/m (antiperiodic)?? That's the BC sign of the Majorana translation, not the spin translation. The spin-space translation T_s (shift of the s labels) has eigenvalues e^{2πij/m} for both sectors (it's a permutation on the spin configs). The flip operator F: F s = −s. "Flip-even/odd sector" = eigenspaces of F: (I±F)/2. The lib's sector_spectra uses P = the flip permutation (flip = (N−1)−arange = the all-bits-complement permutation = the global spin flip ✓). The "momentum" for the closed form: the flip-even sector uses k=(2j−1)π/m — that's the momentum of a fermionic object with antiperiodic BC. In the spin space, the natural basis is the Fourier transform of the translation; the flip-even/odd sectors decompose under the translation into even/odd subsets of the Fourier modes... the 2×2 block structure arises because C (a product of E and D_h, each a sum of a "bulk" + a "wrap" term) in the Fourier basis couples q and −q... the (q,−q) pair block! (From the dead-end note: "use the (q,−q) JW pair blocks"). So: on the sector ε, C is block-diagonal in the (q, −q) pairs (q ≠ 0, π), with a 2×2 block for each pair, and a 1×1 block for q=0 (and q=π if present). The 2×2 block's eigenvalues = w⁺, w⁻. The q=0 block's eigenvalue = w_0^+ or w_0^- depending on the sector/parity — and the parity-selection rule (even/odd number of "−") determines which combination appears in which flip sector. The script: build C (dense, m=4..7), project to the flip sector ε, diagonalize with a unitary Fourier transform over the translation (the spin configs are not translation-invariant vectors — the Fourier basis for functions on the spin config: the translation operator T on C^{2^m} (the shift of the spin indices) is unitary and diagonalizable in the Fourier basis over the characters of Z_m × Z_2^m... the spin space C^{2^m} = ℓ²({±1}^m); the translation (Ts)k = s{k+1} (with s_{m+1}=s_1) is a permutation of the 2^m configs? No! The shift of a cyclic spin config maps a config to a config — yes, it's a permutation of the 2^m states (a rotation of the ring). Its eigenvectors: the Fourier transform over the rotation group: decomposes into invariant subspaces indexed by the orbit structure — not simply m-dimensional. The free-fermion diagonalization isn't in the plain spin Fourier basis; it's in the JW (Majorana) basis. OK — I'll implement the JW embedding in the script (I've done similar before; the bit-order pitfall is documented: "spin s_k must be 1−2·bit_{m−1−k} to match the kron ordering"). The embedding: an occupation n_k ∈ {0,1}, s_k = 1−2n_k; parity ε = ∏(1−2n_k) = (−1)^{Σn_k}·... ∏(1−2n_k) = (−1)^{Σ n_k} (mod) ✓ (each factor is −1 iff n_k=1). Sector ε: Σn_k ≡ (1−ε)/2 mod 2. The Fock-basis construction details:

m spins, sector ε: the configs n ∈ {0,1}^m with Σ n_k ≡ (1−ε)/2 (mod 2). Order the basis by integer value of n (bit k−1 = n_k, k=1..m — pick the convention and stick to it; the script is self-consistent, and the spin matrix is built in the same convention).
E_spin[s',s] (the spin basis, the same integer convention: s_k = 1−2n_k).
E_Fock = E_spin (the same matrix, the basis relabeled n↔s) — wait: is the basis order consistent? The spin basis is ordered by the integer n_s = Σ (1−(s_k+1)/2) 2^{k−1} = Σ n_k 2^{k−1} — the spin-integer index = the n-integer index under s_k=1−2n_k. So yes: E_Fock = E_spin, literally the same array, the rows/columns interpreted as the n-basis.
The fermion operators in the Fock basis: for a_k: matrix A_k[n',n]: nonzero when n' = n⊕(e_k) (a flip of bit k−1) — a_k annihilates: n_k=1 → n'=n−e_k, amplitude (−1)^{Σ_{j<k} n_j}; a_k†: n_k=0 → n'=n+e_k, amplitude (−1)^{Σ_{j<k} n_j}. Note: this flips the parity → A_k maps the sector ε to −ε!! In the fixed-parity Fock space, a single a_k doesn't live in the sector — but a quadratic a†k a{k+1} does. So the space of monomials: I fit E_Fock (sector→sector) as a sum of even monomials: the number terms a†k a_k (m), the NN hopping a†k a{k+1} + h.c. (m, including the PBC k=m→1), the pairing a†k a†{k+1} + h.c. (m, including the PBC). Total 3m monomials (each h.c. pair = one real coefficient... but the coefficients can be complex; use 2m complex monomials for the hopping/pairing + m real for the number).   NSAMP = {}
  def set_nsamp(D):
      NSAMP.clear()
      for (L, p), (data, rec) in D.items():
          NSAMP[(L, p)] = 1.0 / len(data)
  def _floor(L, p):
      return NSAMP.get((L, p), 1e-6)

Extra section 8 code:



  # ------------------------------------------------------------------ 8. v14 additions
  from scipy import stats as _stats
  PC14 = 0.1597
  print("\n== 8. v14: alpha at adopted p_c = 0.1597, s1L^-y test, chi2 p-values, floor audit ==")
  v14 = {}
  ps_s8 = [p for p in ps_all if 0.145 <= p <= 0.175]
  arows = {}
  for tf in (4.0, 2.0):
      for Lmin in (16, 32, 48, 64):
          Lset = [L for L in Ls_all if has_t(L, tf) and L >= Lmin]
          Luse = [L for L in Lset if all((L, p) in D for p in (0.15, 0.155, 0.16, 0.165, 0.17))]
          M8 = means(D, Luse, ps_s8, COL["S_half"], tf)
          a, b, c2 = log_slope_interp(M8, Luse, ps_s8, PC14)
          bs = [log_slope_interp(means(D, Luse, ps_s8, COL["S_half"], tf, rng), Luse, ps_s8, rng.normal(PC14, 0.0004))[0] for _ in range(NB)]
          arows[f"t={tf:g}L;L>={Lmin}"] = dict(alpha=float(a), se=float(np.std(bs, ddof=1)), chi2dof=float(c2), Ls=Luse)
          print(f"  alpha at p_c=0.1597, t={tf:g}L, L>={Lmin}: {a:.4f} ± {np.std(bs, ddof=1):.4f}  chi2/dof={c2:.2f}")
  # s1 L^-y correction test (AIC)
  s1 = {}
  for tf in (4.0, 2.0):
      for Lmin in (32, 64):
          Lset = [L for L in Ls_all if has_t(L, tf) and L >= Lmin]
          Luse = [L for L in Lset if all((L, p) in D for p in (0.15, 0.155, 0.16, 0.165, 0.17))]
          M8 = means(D, Luse, ps_s8, COL["S_half"], tf)
          x = np.log(np.array(Luse, float))
          y8 = np.array([CubicSpline([p for p in ps_s8 if (L, p) in M8], [M8[(L, p)][0] for p in ps_s8 if (L, p) in M8])(PC14) for L in Luse])
          e8 = np.array([CubicSpline([p for p in ps_s8 if (L, p) in M8], [M8[(L, p)][1] for p in ps_s8 if (L, p) in M8])(PC14) for L in Luse])
          n8 = len(x)
          def _fit3(Linv_y, use_corr):
              Vm = np.vstack([x, np.ones_like(x)] + ([np.array(Luse, float) ** -Inv_y] if use_corr else [])).T
              W = 1 / e8
              coef, *_ = np.linalg.lstsq(Vm * W[:, None], y8 * W, rcond=None)
              r = (Vm @ coef - y8) * W
              ch2 = float((r * r).sum()); k = Vm.shape[1]
              return ch2, k, coef[0]
          for Inv_y, ylab in ((0.5, "0.5"), (1.0, "1"), (2.0, "2")):
              ch0, k0, a0 = _fit3(ylab, False); ch1, k1, a1 = _fit3(ylab, True)
              dAIC = 2 * (k1 - k0) - 2 * (ch0 - ch1)
              s1[f"t={tf:g}L;L>={Lmin};y={ylab}"] = dict(dAIC=float(dAIC), d_alpha=float(a1 - a0))
              print(f"  s1 test t={tf:g}L L>={Lmin} y={ylab}: dAIC={dAIC:+.2f}  d_alpha={a1 - a0:+.4f}")
  # chi2 p-values of the windowed collapses
  pv = {}
  for key, row in coll.items():
      dof = row["npts"] - 5
      ch2 = row["chi2dof"] * dof
      pv[key] = float(_stats.chi2.sf(ch2, dof))
  minpv64 = min(v for k, v in pv.items() if "L>=64" in k)
  print(f"  chi2 p-values (windowed collapses): min over L>=64 windows = {minpv64:.3f}")
  v14 = dict(alpha_at_0.1597=arows, s1_test=s1, collapse_chi2_pvalues=pv, min_pv_L64=minpv64,
             floor_audit=...)
Floor-binding audit: for each window (tf, Lmin, xmax): the set of points (L,p) in the window (x=(p−pc)L^{1/ν} ≤ xmax, using the final pc, ν): check min se vs 1e-6 (the old floor) and 1/N:



  faudit = []
  for key, row in coll.items():
      pc, nu = row["pc"], row["nu"]; Lmin = int(key.split("L>=")[1].split(";")[0]); xmax = float(key.split("xmax=")[1]); tf = float(key.split("t=")[1].split("L")[0])
      worst = None
      for L in Ls_all:
          if L < Lmin or not has_t(L, tf): continue
          for p in ps_all:
              if (L, p) not in D: continue
              if 0.13 > p or p > 0.19: continue
              x = (p - pc) * L ** (1 / nu)
              if abs(x) > xmax: continue
              data, rec = D[(L, p)]; t = int(round(tf * L)); it = list(rec).index(t)
              se = float(data[:, it, COL["I3"]].std(ddof=1) / np.sqrt(len(data)))
              N = len(data); fl = 1.0 / N
              bind = se < 1e-6 or (se < fl)
              if worst is None or se < worst[0]: worst = (se, L, p, N, fl, bind)
      faudit.append(dict(window=key, min_se=worst[0], at=f"L={worst[1]},p={worst[2]}", N=worst[3], floor_1N=worst[4], binds_old=worst[0] < 1e-6, binds_new=worst[5]))
      print(f"  floor audit {key}: min se in window = {worst[0]:.2e} (L={worst[1]}, p={worst[2]}), 1/N = {worst[4]:.2e} -> binds old 1e-6: {worst[0] < 1e-6}, binds new 1/N: {worst[5]}")
  v14["floor_audit"] = faudit
  out["v14"] = v14

python


import re
src = open('mipt_fss2.py').read()
# 1) insert NSAMP machinery after the import line "from mipt_fss import ..."
anchor = "from mipt_fss import means, COL, log_slope, log_slope_interp, fit_s_collapse\n"
assert anchor in src
mach = anchor + '''
# v14 (item 13): standard-error floor of 1/N_traj per (L,p) instead of 1e-4/1e-6.
# NSAMP is filled by set_nsamp(D) (called by the analysis driver); without it the
# legacy 1e-4/1e-6 behaviour is retained.
NSAMP = {}
def set_nsamp(D):
    NSAMP.clear()
    for (L, p), (data, rec) in D.items():
        NSAMP[(L, p)] = 1.0 / len(data)
def _floor(L, p):
    return NSAMP.get((L, p), 1e-4)
'''
src = src.replace(anchor, mach, 1)
# 2) local_crossing floor
old = "e = np.array([max(np.hypot(M[(L1, p)][1], M[(L2, p)][1]), 1e-4) for p in ps])"
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, "e = np.array([max(np.hypot(M[(L1, p)][1], M[(L2, p)][1]), max(_floor(L1, p), _floor(L2, p))) for p in ps])", 1)
Hmm wait — local_crossing uses hypot(se1, se2); the 1/N floor should be applied per point: floor = max(1/N(L1,p), 1/N(L2,p))? The se = hypot(se1,se2) already incorporates both; the floor should be ≥ each 1/N → max. OK.



# 3) slope_at floor
old = "y = np.array([M[(L, p)][0] for p in pp]); e = np.array([max(M[(L, p)][1], 1e-4) for p in pp])"
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, "y = np.array([M[(L, p)][0] for p in pp]); e = np.array([max(M[(L, p)][1], _floor(L, p)) for p in pp])", 1)
# 4) collapse_cost floor
old = "m, se = M[(L, p)]; xs.append(x); ys.append(m); ws.append(1 / max(se, 1e-6) ** 2)"
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, "m, se = M[(L, p)]; xs.append(x); ys.append(m); ws.append(1 / max(se, _floor(L, p)) ** 2)", 1)
# 5) standalone main: set NSAMP
old = '    D = load()\n    D = {k: v for k, v in D.items() if k[0] <= 256}'
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, "    D = load()\n    set_nsamp(D)\n    D = {k: v for k, v in D.items() if k[0] <= 256}", 1)
# 6) header docstring note
open('mipt_fss2_floorN.py','w').write(src.replace('"""Definitive FSS analysis (v2):', '"""Definitive FSS analysis (v2, v14 floorN):', 1))

"\subsection{Collatz--Wielandt enclosures of the two-replica Perron eigenvalue} \label{app:enclosures}

The statements above concern the $n=3$ operator. Now — the content of the proposition and proof. Let me draft it now (to finalize after the script confirms the factorization + factor):

\begin{proposition}[Closed form of the nonzero spectrum of the compressed two-replica operator] \label{prop:spectrum} Let $L=2m\ge4$, $d\ge2$, $0<p<1$, and let $\Ccomp=\mathcal W_0^L E D_h E^{\mathsf T}D_h$ be the operator of Eq.\eqref{eq:Isingform}, with $K_d,K_h,\mathcal W_0$ as in Eq.\eqref{eq:Kdh}. For $k\in[0,2\pi)$ define \begin{equation} Q(k)=\cosh^2 2K_d,\cosh 2K_h+\sinh^2 2K_d,\sinh 2K_h-\sinh 2K_h\cos k,\qquad R(k)=2\sinh 2K_d,\cos\frac{k}{2}, \label{eq:QRdef} \end{equation} and $w_k^\pm=Q(k)\pm\sqrt{Q(k)^2-R(k)^2}$ (real, since $0\le R^2\le Q^2$ for all $k$ verify: is Q²−R² ≥ 0 always? at k=0: Q(0)=cosh²2Kd cosh2Kh + sinh²2Kd sinh2Kh + sinh2Kh; R(0)=2sinh2Kd. Q²−R² ≥ 0? The lib uses max(Q²−R², 0) — suggesting it can go slightly negative numerically; analytically it should be ≥0. Hmm — is Q²≥R² always? For the triangular lattice free-fermion, the "gap" Q²−R²... at k=π: Q(π)=cosh²2Kd cosh2Kh + sinh²2Kd sinh2Kh + sinh2Kh > R(π)=0 ✓. Now (E τ){s's} = exp[Kd Σ_k s'k s{k+1} + s'k s{k+2}] = relabeling (k→k−1, then k→k−2): = exp[Kd Σ_k s'{k+1} s_k + s'{k+1} s{k+1}] = exp[Kd Σ_k s_k s'{k+1} + s'{k+1}s_{k+1}] — vs Eᵀ = exp[Kd Σ_k s_k s'k + s_k s'{k+1}]: (E τ) has the terms s_k s'{k+1} + s{k+1} s'{k+1} = s'{k+1}(s_k+s_{k+1}); Eᵀ has s_k(s'k + s'{k+1}). Not equal. So E τ ≠ Eᵀ. What about τ E? (τE){s's} = exp[Kd Σ_k s'{k+1}(s_k + s_{k+1})] = s'{k+1} s_k + s'{k+1}s_{k+1} — same as (Eτ)! So E τ = τ E (E is shift-covariant: it commutes with a simultaneous shift) ✓ — and E τ ≠ Eᵀ. Then the rank theorem's claim "E^T has exactly the M2 structure" — M2_{s's} = ∏ w(s'k | s{k−1}, s_k): (E τ){s's} = exp[Kd Σ_k s'k (s{k+1}+s_k)] hmm (Eτ){s's} = E_{s', τ^{-1}s} = exp[Kd Σ_k s'k (s{k+1} + s_{k+2})]... ugh, I keep making mistakes. (τ^{-1}s)k = s{(k−1) mod m +1} = s_{k+1} (indices mod m, 1-based: (τ^{-1}s)k = s{k+1}). Then (E τ){s's} = Σ_k s'k[(τ^{-1}s)k + (τ^{-1}s){k+1}] = Σ_k s'k(s{k+1} + s{k+2}). M2 needs Σ_k s'k(s{k−1} + s_k). relabeling (Eτ)'s sum: k→k−1: Σ_k s'{k+1}(s_k+s_{k+1}) — the s' index is shifted: that's (τ E){s's}... (τE){s's} = E_{τ^{-1}s', s} = Σ_k (τ^{-1}s')k(s_k+s{k+1}) = Σ_k s'{k+1}(s_k+s{k+1}) ✓ same. So E τ = τ E (the covariance ✓), and M2 = E with the output shifted = τ E (in the row space) = E τ. So M2's "Eᵀ" in the theorem = E τ (the theorem writes E^T as an abbreviation for the transposed matrix, which equals E τ: is Eᵀ (the matrix transpose) = E τ? Equal! So Eᵀ = M2's E = E τ (as a matrix) ✓ (the relabeling works because the ring is symmetric). Good: Eᵀ = E τ = τ E, where τ is the ring shift (both the row and column shifts... note: E τ = τ E means E commutes with a simultaneous shift; Eᵀ (the matrix transpose) equals that common value). So C = W₀^L E D_h Eᵀ D_h = W₀^L E D_h E τ D_h. And (E D_h)² = E D_h E D_h — C = (E D_h)² with an extra τ D_h D_h^{-1}: C = E D_h E τ D_h = E D_h E D_h τ D_h^{-2} D_h... D_h τ = τ D_h (D_h is shift-invariant ✓): C = E D_h E τ D_h = E D_h τ E D_h (if E τ = τ E ✓) = (E D_h)² with the middle τ... E D_h τ E D_h = E D_h E D_h (τ in the middle) ≠ (E D_h)² unless [D_h, τ]=0 — it is zero! D_h τ = τ D_h, so E D_h τ E D_h = E τ D_h E D_h = τ E D_h E D_h (Eτ=τE) = τ (E D_h)². So C = W₀^L τ (E D_h)². The v2 doc says "C_comp = W₀^L (E D_h)² τ" — τ commutes with C (C τ = τ C? C τ = τ (E D_h)² τ = τ (E D_h τ) (τ^{-1} E D_h) τ... probably ✓), so the ordering is immaterial. Good: C = W₀^L τ G², G := E D_h, and τ G² = G² τ (G = E D_h: G τ = E D_h τ = E τ D_h = τ E D_h = τ G ✓). So C = W₀^L G² τ = W₀^L τ G² — and G² τ: G² and τ commute → C's spectrum = W₀^L × the spectrum of G² τ. G² τ = (G τ^{1/2})²... not a square (τ^{1/2} is not an operator). But G τ G = G·τ·G: (G τ G) and G²: G τ G = G (τ G) = G (G τ) = G² τ ✓ trivial. The nonzero spectrum of G² τ = the spectrum of (G τ^{1/2})(G τ^{1/2}) — hmm, τ (the shift, a permutation of order m) has a "square root" only on the subspaces where... the standard trick: G² τ has the same nonzero eigenvalues as (G τ)(G τ)?? (Gτ)² = G τ G τ = G (τ G) τ = G (G τ) τ = G² τ² = G² (if m|2)... no, τ² ≠ 1. Hmm: the nonzero spectrum of G² τ equals the nonzero spectrum of (Gτ)(τ^{-1}Gτ)... a similarity: G² τ = G (G τ) = G (τ G) — and τ^{-1}(G² τ)τ = (τ^{-1}Gτ)² — not the same. A cleaner approach: the nonzero eigenvalues of G² τ: the nonzero eigenvalues of AB = the nonzero eigenvalues of BA: take A = G², B = τ: the nonzero spec of G² τ = the nonzero spec of τ G² (same) — the τ G² τ^{-1} trick: τ G² τ^{-1} = (τ G τ^{-1})² — and τ G τ^{-1} = τ E D_h τ^{-1} = (τ E τ^{-1})(τ D_h τ^{-1}) = E' D_h, E' = τ E τ^{-1}: (τEτ^{-1}){s's} = E{τ^{-1}s', τ s} = expKd Σ_k s'{k+1}... So τ G τ^{-1} = G!! (G commutes with τ — I showed Gτ=τG ✓). So τ G² τ^{-1} = G² — the similarity is trivial. Useless. The right tool: the nonzero spectrum of G² τ = the spectrum of (G τ)^{...} — note: G² τ = G (G τ); the nonzero spectrum of the product of G and (Gτ) = the nonzero spectrum of (Gτ) G = G τ G = G² τ (cyclic) — circular. OK, the standard free-fermion result: G (the one-row transfer of the triangular lattice with a spinor) — in the Kaufman spinor representation, G is represented by a 2^m × 2^m matrix, which (after the spinor/Fourier transform) block-diagonalizes into 2×2 blocks per k, and τ (the shift) acts as a phase e^{ik} in the k-block → G² τ in the block = (2×2 G block)² × e^{iφ_k}... the eigenvalues = the square of the block's eigenvalues × the twist phase — and the w_k^± product = ∏ (the per-mode block eigenvalues of G)² · phase — consistent with the formula λ = (2W₀²)^m ∏ w_k^± (the per-mode contribution w_k^{±} = the square of the mode block eigenvalue, up to the phase, which is absorbed in the even/odd "−" count!). The even/odd "−" parity rule = the phase bookkeeping (the sign of the twist per sector) — the k=0 sign flip at p_c = the change of the phase convention. [blocked]

So the formal proof: (i) the spinor representation: represent the 2-state (spin) transfer G = E D_h as a 4-state (spinor = "two Majoranas per site" / "the even+odd combination") transfer — the standard Kaufman spinor: the site has two states (spin ±), the spinor has two components (a,b); the one-row transfer G (2^m × 2^m) embeds into a 4^m spinor space... actually Kaufman's method: the triangular-lattice transfer (a 2-state output spin coupled to a 2-state input pair) is exactly a product of 2×2 "spinor matrices" in a 4-state space, so its trace/determinant = a product of 2×2 matrices — and G² τ (the "square" = the two-row transfer = one period) is a product of the spinor matrices around the ring → the eigenvalues = the products around the ring = the ∏ w_k^± in the Fourier (spinor momentum) space.

(i) The identities Eᵀ = Eτ = τE, D_h τ = τ D_h, hence C = W₀^L G² τ, G = E D_h (all verified numerically). 2. (iv) The sector/twist rule: the spinor PBC: the ring monodromy of the spinor string gives the BC sign = (−1)^{m+1}·ε_flip or similar — empirically: the flip-even sector carries the antiperiodic k=(2j−1)π/m, the flip-odd the periodic 2πj/m; the parity (even/odd count of "−") = the choice of the eigenvalue branch of M_ε(k) consistent with the fermion number; the k=0 mode at p_c: w_0^- → 0, and the branch assignment flips (the even/odd rule) — the script verifies the rule at pc2±2e-4. 5. (v) The kernel: at k=π, w_π^− = 0 → the mode count → the rank 3·2^{m−2} ✓. 6. mipt_audit3_gauss_proof.py — full design:

python


"""v14: numerical verification of every step of the closed-form spectrum Proposition (proof-to-code chain).
C = C_comp = W0^L E D_h E^T D_h,  E_{s's} = exp[K_d sum_k s'_k (s_k + s_{k+1})],  D_h = diag exp[K_h sum_k s_k s_{k+1}], spins in {±1}^m, PBC.
Outputs: mipt_results/audit3_gauss_proof.json, logs/mipt_audit3_gauss_proof.log
"""
import numpy as np, json, os, sys, functools, time, itertools, math
print = functools.partial(print, flush=True)
from mipt_kaufman_lib import couplings, QR, logw, spectrum_closed, gaps, PC2
OUT = "mipt_results"; LOG = "logs/mipt_audit3_gauss_proof.log"
os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True); os.makedirs("logs", exist_ok=True)
LOGF = open(LOG, "w")
def log(*a):
    s = " ".join(str(x) for x in a)
    print(s); LOGF.write(s + "\n"); LOGF.flush()
def spins(m):
    # index n -> spin tuple (s_1..s_m), s_k = +1 - 2*bit_{k-1}
    N = 2 ** m
    S = np.zeros((m, N), dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(m):
        S[k] = 1.0 - 2.0 * (((np.arange(N) >> k) & 1))
    return S
def build_EDh(m, Kd, Kh):
    S = spins(m)
    # row spins S' = S (same index space), column spins S
    # E[s', s] = exp[Kd * sum_k S[k, s'] * (S[k, s] + S[(k+1)%m, s])]
    # build sum over k: for each k: Kd * outer(S[k], S[k] + S[(k+1)%m])
    acc = np.zeros((2**m, 2**m))
    for k in range(m):
        acc += np.outer(S[k], S[k] + S[(k+1) % m])
    E = np.exp(Kd * acc)
    Dh = np.exp(Kh * sum(S[k] * S[(k+1) % m] for k in range(m)))
    return E, np.diag(Dh)
def build_C(m, p, d, W0):
    Kd, Kh, W0l = couplings(p, d)
    E, Dh = build_EDh(m, Kd, Kh)
    return (W0l ** (2*m)) * E @ Dh @ E.T @ Dh   # C_comp = W0^L E D_h E^T D_h, L=2m
Wait — I need to double-check which W0 power: C_comp = W0^L E D_h Eᵀ D_h, and couplings() returns W0 = (ac b²)^{1/4} = W0 from the manuscript (Eq. Kdh: 𝒲₀=(acb²)^{1/4}) ✓. L = 2m. So C = W0^{2m} E Dh Eᵀ Dh. And the lib's λ₁ formula: (2W0²)^m ∏ w_k^+ — compare λ₁(C) with that: ratio = W0^{2m}·∏... vs (2W0²)^m∏ = 2^m W0^{2m} ∏ — so the lib formula = 2^m × (my C)? Unless the lib's dense() builds C without W0 (i.e., E D_h Eᵀ D_h): in that case λ₁(lib C) = (2W0²)^m ∏ w_k^+ → λ₁(my C) = W0^{2m}(2W0²)^m ∏. The script will compute both and record the exact ratio (must be an exact power of 2·W0 — verify numerically). In the Proposition, state: "nonzero eigenvalues of C_comp: λ = W0^L (2W0²)^m ∏ w_k^{±}" — check which is correct, and write the factor accordingly. Hmm, wait — actually, let me reconsider: does the lib's dense() include W0? make_ops(m, d, p) from z2_gap_scan — I'd need to look at that code to know. The lib's header says "lambda_1 to 3e-14 at L=32" compared with "sparse power iteration" (presumably of the true C_comp including W0, from mipt_benchmarks). If the lib's spectrum_closed matched the true C_comp's λ₁ (the benchmark values, e.g., p=0.40, L=28: λ₁^{1/28}=0.6838449), then spectrum_closed includes the correct overall factor for C_comp. The v2 document states "λ = (2 W₀²)^m ∏ w_k^±" as the verified statement for C_comp. So let's verify: C_comp = W0^L E D_h Eᵀ D_h; my script computes C_comp densely and compares with (2W0²)^m ∏ — if the ratio is exactly 1, the formula is correct as is; if the ratio is 2^m, adjust. The script prints the ratio for a few configurations; I'll write the Proposition accordingly (the ratio should be exactly 1 or 2^m — a power of 2 — since W0 cancels… no, the ratio is (eigenvalue of C_comp)/(lib product) — since C_comp = W0^L (ED_hEᵀD_h) and the lib product is for ED_hEᵀD_h or for C_comp, the ratio = W0^{±L}·2^{±m}… W0 is not a power of 2 in general (W0=(acb²)^{1/4} ~ 0.2-0.6), so the ratio will pin down the factor exactly).

S1 (Gaussianity of E in the Fock basis): E is a "classical" matrix (function of spins only) — the Fock/JW quadratic representation: I decided this gets complicated. A simpler and more faithful plan (aligned with the route and the v2 document): verify the spinor factorization E = coshK₂ Ỹ + sinhK₂ s_m Ỹ s_1 (S2) with a parameter search — if it passes, the proof uses it. Plus: S3: Ỹ = c e^{K₁ s_m} U_{m−1}⋯U_1, U_k = e^{K₂ s_k s_{k+1}} c e^{K₁ s_k} — the factorization is Ỹ itself; the residual of S2 covers it. S4: Determinant identity: det(E_ε) — compute E_ε (sector restriction), det^{1/2^{m−1}}, compare with ρ_ε c^m, ρ_ε² = 1 + ε(−1)^m (sinh2K₂/sinh2K₁)^m (with the same K₁,K₂ as S2). S5: Full spectrum by sector (core): for m=4..9, d∈{2,3,5}, p grid: the nonzero eigenvalues of C (via eigvals of C — C is not symmetric! C = W0^L E D_h Eᵀ D_h — is it symmetric? S9: λ₁ at L=28,32 (m=14,16): power iteration of C_comp = W0^L E D_h Eᵀ D_h — matrix-free matvec: C v = W0^L E (Dh (Eᵀ (Dh v))). The matvec of E: (E u)s' = Σ_s exp[Kd Σ_k s'k(s_k+s{k+1})] Dh(s) u_s — compute efficiently: for fixed s', the sum over s: group by (s_k+s{k+1})… simpler: E u = exp(Kd·M @ s_col ...) — E is a "classical" matrix: E_{s's} = exp(Kd Σ_k Sk,s' [blocked]) — matvec: for each s: weight = Dh(s) u_s · exp(Kd Σ_k Sk,s [blocked]) (a function of s only) → (E (Dh u)){s'} = Σ_s exp(Kd Σ_k S[k,s'] S[k,s]) · w_s — that's like a Walsh–Hadamard (Hadamard!) transform: exp(Kd Σ_k S[k,s'] S[k,s]) = a product of 1D factors over k → a fast Hadamard transform! fhat = FWT of (w_s · ...). Actually: (E v){s'} = Σ_s [Π_k e^{Kd s'k s_k}] v_s — a separable kernel = the unnormalized Walsh–Hadamard transform (Hadamard matrix H with H{s's} = Π_k e^{Kd s'k s_k} — a rank-1 product structure: H = Π over k of (a Kronecker factor) — H = K_1 ⊗ K_2 ⊗ ... ⊗ K_m, where (K){ab} = e^{Kd a b} (a,b∈{±1}) (2×2). So E v = (⊗K) v — computable in O(m 2^m) with an iterated 2×2 transform! Similarly Eᵀ = the same matrix with the index roles reversed = also ⊗K (K is symmetric: K_{ab} = e^{Kd ab} — symmetric ✓, so E = ⊗k K is actually a Kronecker product of m identical 2×2 matrices!! Wait: E{s's} = exp[Kd Σ_k s'_k s_k]·exp[Kd Σ_k s'k s{k+1}] — two factors: the first is ⊗K (the s'k s_k coupling), the second is a different structure (s'k couples to s{k+1} — shifted): the second factor = ⊗K' with K'{s'_k, s_k} = e^{Kd s'k s{k+1}} — that's a shift of the column index: = (⊗K) with the column permuted by the ring shift τ. So E = (⊗K) P_τ, where P_τ is the ring-shift permutation of the column index! Hence E matvec = P_τᵀ ((⊗K) v) = shift(FWT_K(v)) — O(m 2^m) ✓. And D_h is a diagonal O(2^m). So C v = W0^L E Dh Eᵀ Dh v: 4 passes × O(m 2^m) — m=16: 16·65536 ≈ 1M operations per pass — fast. Main parts:

The build_EDh function (above).
The sector projection: F permutation: f(n) = (N−1)−n. Build P as a 2^{m−1}×2^{m} selection matrix: the indices with parity +1 (Σ bits even): the eigenvalue of F on the basis vector e_n: F e_n = e_{N−1−n}. Sector +1 (the flip-even subspace, P=(I+F)/2): the eigenvectors are (e_n + e_{N−1−n})/√2 (n and N−1−n are distinct except when n = N−1−n → impossible (2n = N−1 is odd)). The C|sector eigenvalues = the eigenvalues of C restricted: build the sector matrix: B = C[rows in the sector representative]... C commutes with F (verify), so C is block-diagonal in the (e_n ± e_{N−1−n})/√2 basis: for a pair {n, N−1−n}: the 2×2 block [[C_nn + C_{n,n'}, C_{n,n'}+C_{n,n}], ...] — simpler: use numpy: build the unitary U (2^m × 2^m) with columns (e_n±e_{f(n)})/√2; D = Uᵀ C U → block-diagonal with 2×2 blocks (some 1×1). Extract the blocks → per-sector eigenvalues = the eigenvalues of the 2×2 blocks. Or even simpler: the eigenvalues of C P_+ (the product) — the nonzero part: use the 2×2 block extraction (also verifies the block structure!). For the spectrum comparison (S5), I'll extract the 2×2 blocks: for each orbit pair {n, f(n)} (n < f(n)): block B = [[(C_nn + C_{n f})/√2, (C_{n f} + C_{n n})/√2]... let me derive: basis vectors v_+ = (e_n + e_{f})/√2, v_− = (e_n − e_f)/√2. C v_+ = (C e_n + C e_f)/√2. Write C e_n = a e_n + b e_f + (rest in other pairs — if [C,F]=0, the rest has zero overlap with v_±): (C e_n + C e_f)·v_+ = (a + C_{f n} + ... )... let's just do it numerically with the full unitary transform: U (2^m × 2^m) built from the pair basis; M = U.T @ C @ U (C is real, not symmetric — use U.T? U is a real orthogonal matrix (the columns are orthonormal: the v_+ for distinct pairs are orthogonal ✓) → M = U.T C U; M should be block-diagonal with 2×2 blocks (verify the off-block norm is small). Eigenvalues of each 2×2 block = the sector spectrum. This directly verifies "C is block-diagonal in the flip-pair basis with 2×2 blocks" (the S10b check) and gives the sector eigenvalues. Note: m can be odd (m=5,7,9: n=4..): the pairs are still well-defined (f(n)≠n always holds) ✓.
